# Initiative #116 — cua compatibility surface inventory

Owner boundary (2026-10-14): reproduce the first-call computer/browser contract on **macOS and Linux**, plus **all 147 members of the browser manifest (22 interfaces, 66 named types)** and the eight advertised capability families. A provider-default gate or `documented:false` is **not an exclusion**: retain the implementation and its feature-detection behavior; expose it only when the trusted service advertises support. `iab`, `mcpapps`, Windows and vendor cloud/account/telemetry implementations are out. Browser `browserAuth`/OTP, `botDetection`, browser history, management browser **windows**, CDP, WebMCP, and generic DOM-only tabs remain in; “Windows” the OS is not browser windows.

**Count: 222 included member-contract rows vs 16 excluded member-contract rows** (147 included browser-manifest members, **0 excluded browser-manifest members**; 103 included type rows, including all 66 manifest types). Count convention: method and property declarations count once; Target inheritance is one shared declaration plus App override; aliases `cua.browsers`/BrowserProvider/first-call Browser and lifecycle signatures point to canonical manifest members rather than duplicate them. The two first-call-documented host nodeRepl output members count (separate from cua); capability collections/base members and returned WebMcpTools members count; management has four namespace properties and four index-signature dispatch contracts, not a fictional finite list of every Chrome method. Excluded-member count is 4 legacy-root members + 12 Windows App implementations; type variants, docs, package paths and dependency families are counted separately as rows, not inflated into members.

Sources are read-only **chatgpt-26.928.40906**, rooted at `/Users/new/codex-app-src/readable/chatgpt-26.928.40906/`; citations below retain build + file + readable line. Input research: `/Users/new/.claude/jobs/1ea561bc/tmp/116-research-A.txt`. Table 1 quotes declared vendor signatures (implementation-inferred helpers are identified) except explicitly narrowed platform/provider literals; Table 2 gives precise deletions/rebrands; Table 3 is the text edit plan (mixed sections are split so the surviving prose stays verbatim). Product-name replacement is prose-only, never automatic renaming of JS identifiers, protocol fields, service names, or vendor URLs. No undocumented direct raw Sky method is added to the model contract merely because the current computer implementation is a Sky proxy; the documented computer members and all bound App methods above define this boundary. Static inspection only: no runtime, Chrome, accounts, configuration or secrets files were opened. Included service labels describe ultimate calls: `sky` native trusted service, `browser` trusted browser service, `none` local value/constructor/declaration.

Common browser-client requirements: Runtime proxy (browser-client.js:10061-10212) must hide disabled members as undefined and from in/ownKeys/descriptors, wrap returned handles and unwrap API arguments. Gating is per browser; inclusion here does not force availability. Transport is `nodeRepl.rpc("browser", {method:"setup"|"execute"|"executeWithRecovery", params:...})`; Sky stays behind `nodeRepl.rpc("sky",...)`, not a second native engine (browser-client.js:10215-10311; sky.js:95-127). Host output bridges (`nodeRepl.write`/`emitImage`) are included as their own two rows, not new `cua` members; policy/approval remains in the trusted service. No blanket 3-second client timeout or guessed image resizing is introduced.

| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md — On macOS, use `cua.getApp("Example App")` with an app name, path, or bundle ID. On Linux and Windows, use `cua.getApp({ windowId: 123 })` with an exact open window ID from the app inventory. If an app has multiple windows, use their titles to choose the requested one. Do not choose the first window without checking it. | Windows-specific clause in otherwise included prose. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:160` | Delete only Windows clauses / words “and Windows”; preserve macOS/Linux and ordinary browser windows guidance verbatim. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md — `cua.listWindows()` is available on Linux and Windows and includes open windows that have no app entry. If the requested app has no open window, launch its inventory ID with `await cua.computer.launch_app({ app: appId })`, then refresh the inventory and select a window. `getApp` does not launch apps on Linux or Windows. | Windows-specific clause in otherwise included prose. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:162` | Delete only Windows clauses / words “and Windows”; preserve macOS/Linux and ordinary browser windows guidance verbatim. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md — Linux input stays bound to the selected window. Sky sends it without activating that window or moving the desktop pointer. The app can still activate a new window or grab the pointer during a held click, drag, or menu interaction. Coordinates are relative to the selected window. Windows input activates the selected window. Get a fresh Windows screenshot before coordinate actions. The bound app uses that screenshot's coordinate mapping until the next observation; an AX-only observation clears it. | Windows-specific clause in otherwise included prose. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:164` | Delete only Windows clauses / words “and Windows”; preserve macOS/Linux and ordinary browser windows guidance verbatim. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md — Linux and Windows always return full accessibility state. Linux reports the tree source. `at_spi` elements support the actions listed in the tree; `x11` fallback elements are observation-only, so use a screenshot and window-relative coordinates for input. | Windows-specific clause in otherwise included prose. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:170` | Delete only Windows clauses / words “and Windows”; preserve macOS/Linux and ordinary browser windows guidance verbatim. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md — - For `chrome://newtab` (with or without a trailing slash) and Orbit’s signed new-tab extension page, `cua.getTab(...)` displays tab metadata without reading or changing the new-tab page. Use the returned tab's `goto(url)` to navigate to an allowed website. | Vendor brand prose reference (not removal of included capability). | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:175` | Rename vendor product name to cua only; preserve non-excluded prose. Orbit browserAuth remains browserAuth, not vendor account API. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md — - `cua.listWindows()` also displays its result unless `emit: false`. Windows screenshot methods always display images through Sky and reject `emit: false` before capture. They also reject a result with multiple screenshot regions because the bound API returns one image. Sky displays those regions before the error. | Windows-specific clause in otherwise included prose. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:198` | Delete only Windows clauses / words “and Windows”; preserve macOS/Linux and ordinary browser windows guidance verbatim. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md — - macOS app `paste` uses the system pasteboard then restores the user's previous clipboard contents. Linux and Windows app `paste` support only `text` and use the platform's native text input. Browser `paste` does not restore clipboard contents, and its `md` format inserts Markdown source as plain text. Specify `text`, `md`, or `html` explicitly where supported. Prefer `paste` for formatted content and multiline text. | Windows-specific clause in otherwise included prose. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:204` | Delete only Windows clauses / words “and Windows”; preserve macOS/Linux and ordinary browser windows guidance verbatim. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md — - Native app `scroll` accepts a page count on macOS. On Linux, omit the distance for the native default or pass `{ pixels: 500 }`. On Windows, pass a coordinate target and `{ pixels: 500 }`; element targets and page counts are unsupported. Linux element clicks support one left or right click. Use coordinates for other click options. | Windows-specific clause in otherwise included prose. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:205` | Delete only Windows clauses / words “and Windows”; preserve macOS/Linux and ordinary browser windows guidance verbatim. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md — - `selectText` is unavailable on Linux and Windows. `setValue` is unavailable on Linux. These methods throw before sending input. Use the supported bound actions to edit the UI and verify the result. | Windows-specific clause in otherwise included prose. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-cua-repl.md:206` | Delete only Windows clauses / words “and Windows”; preserve macOS/Linux and ordinary browser windows guidance verbatim. |

| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md — On Linux and Windows, select an exact open window ID from the app inventory. If an app has multiple windows, use their titles to choose the requested one. | documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md — Use `cua.listWindows()` to find open windows that have no app entry. If the requested app has no open window, launch its inventory ID with `await cua.computer.launch_app({ app: appId })`, then refresh the inventory and select a window. `getApp` does not launch apps on Linux or Windows. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md:244` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md — Linux input stays bound to the selected window. Sky sends it without activating that window or moving the desktop pointer. The app can still activate a new window or grab the pointer during a held click, drag, or menu interaction. Coordinates are relative to the selected window. Windows input activates the selected window. Get a fresh Windows screenshot before coordinate actions. The bound app uses that screenshot's coordinate mapping until the next observation; an AX-only observation clears it. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md:246` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md — Linux and Windows always return full accessibility state. Linux reports the tree source. `at_spi` elements support the actions listed in the tree; `x11` fallback elements are observation-only, so use a screenshot and window-relative coordinates for input. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md:254` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md — - For `chrome://newtab` (with or without a trailing slash) and Orbit’s signed new-tab extension page, `cua.getTab(...)` displays tab metadata without reading or changing the new-tab page. Use the returned tab's `goto(url)` to navigate to an allowed website. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md:259` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md — - `cua.listWindows()` also displays its result unless `emit: false`. Windows screenshot methods always display images through Sky and reject `emit: false` before capture. They also reject a result with multiple screenshot regions because the bound API returns one image. Sky displays those regions before the error. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md:282` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md — - macOS app `paste` uses the system pasteboard then restores the user's previous clipboard contents. Linux and Windows app `paste` support only `text` and use the platform's native text input. Browser `paste` does not restore clipboard contents, and its `md` format inserts Markdown source as plain text. Specify `text`, `md`, or `html` explicitly where supported. Prefer `paste` for formatted content and multiline text. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md:288` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md — - Native app `scroll` accepts a page count on macOS. On Linux, omit the distance for the native default or pass `{ pixels: 500 }`. On Windows, pass a coordinate target and `{ pixels: 500 }`; element targets and page counts are unsupported. Linux element clicks support one left or right click. Use coordinates for other click options. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md:289` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |
| documentation text edit | docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md — - `selectText` is unavailable on Linux and Windows. `setValue` is unavailable on Linux. These methods throw before sending input. Use the supported bound actions to edit the UI and verify the result. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/docs/tinysky-alt-core-node-repl.md:290` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |

| documentation text edit | references/capabilities/tab/botDetection.md — ## Bot Detection Reporting<br>Use this capability only when the current cloud browser tab is blocked by<br>bot-detection, anti-automation, human-verification, or related access-control<br>systems served by the target site or its anti-bot provider. Report the blocker<br>only after the current URL and visible page provide enough evidence to classify<br>it, then stop or continue according to the surrounding Browser Safety guidance.<br>This report is internal telemetry. It does not replace telling the user that<br>the site blocked this browser or, when appropriate, switching to another<br>reputable source as directed by the Cloud Browser Context. | Cloud-environment assumption in otherwise included capability guidance. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_browser/dist/skill/references/capabilities/tab/botDetection.md:4-12` | Drop cloud-only cross-reference/sentence, preserve the included capability and site-safety rules. |
| documentation text edit | references/capabilities/tab/browserAuth.md — # Tab Capability: browserAuth<br>Collects credentials or an Orbit email OTP for a validated login form and fills them into this tab without returning the values to the caller. Include `submit` only when the page requires an explicit submission action. Omit it for forms that auto-submit during credential entry. | Vendor brand prose reference (not removal of included capability). | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_browser/dist/skill/references/capabilities/tab/browserAuth.md:1-2` | Rename vendor product name to cua only; preserve non-excluded prose. Orbit browserAuth remains browserAuth, not vendor account API. |
| documentation text edit | references/capabilities/tab/browserAuth.md — ## Secure Browser Authentication<br>Read this guidance before beginning sign-in. `browserAuth.request(...)` is the<br>secure sign-in handoff: use it when the user must choose a sign-in method or<br>provide credentials. It can offer the available sign-in methods and collect<br>user-provided credentials directly in a secure ChatGPT form. Browser-client<br>validates, fills, and submits those values without returning them to you. | Vendor brand prose reference (not removal of included capability). | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_browser/dist/skill/references/capabilities/tab/browserAuth.md:4-9` | Rename vendor product name to cua only; preserve non-excluded prose. Orbit browserAuth remains browserAuth, not vendor account API. |
| documentation text edit | references/capabilities/tab/browserAuth.md — 1. Before the first authentication interaction, retain the target site's origin<br>   or a canonical signed-in URL for later verification, for example with<br>   `const targetOrigin = new URL(await tab.url()).origin`.<br>2. Inspect the visible page. List only methods the<br>   page actually offers, such as phone or SMS OTP, email or Gmail OTP, Google<br>   sign-in, username and password, passkey, or device approval. If exactly one<br>   method is available, tell the user which method the website offers and<br>   proceed without asking them to choose. If multiple methods are available,<br>   call `browserAuth.request(...)` with a separate, clearly labeled option for<br>   each visible method and wait for the user to choose. Include `options` only<br>   when the user must choose between two or more visible sign-in methods. Set<br>   each option's `label` to just the short visible method, such as "Google",<br>   "mobile number", or "email and password". Labels complete the phrase<br>   "Continue with {label}," so choose a label that makes sense in that context.<br>   Use the same secure request to collect any already-visible credential fields<br>   required by the selected method. Never ask for credentials through chat or<br>   another tool.<br>   Describe a saved-account method with the visible account name, email, or<br>   provider when the page identifies it; never describe it only as "Password for<br>   saved account." Do not infer account details that are not visible or rank or<br>   choose a method for the user.<br>3. Follow the selected method through the visible page. Use<br>   `browserAuth.request(...)` for sign-in method choices and whenever the<br>   chosen method requires user-provided credentials.<br>   If the website displays a QR code for approval on another device, call<br>   `browserAuth.request({ origin: new URL(await tab.url()).origin, fields: [], qr_code: true })`.<br>   Browser-client securely captures and decodes the visible QR code. Never<br>   inspect, print, copy, or reconstruct its destination URL yourself. The only<br>   exception is a trusted native-mobile handoff error that explicitly provides<br>   a validated HTTPS sign-in URL: show the user that exact supplied URL, ask<br>   them to open it and report back when finished, and wait for their reply.<br>   Never expose another QR payload or derive a URL the error did not supply.<br>   If two-step verification or device approval displays a number-matching<br>   challenge, tell the user the exact non-secret number or matching detail to<br>   select on their device, for example, "Tap 37 on your phone."<br>   If the matching detail is an emoji, icon, or image, describe that exact<br>   visual cue, for example, "Tap the 🥶 emoji on your phone." Never invent a<br>   number or translate an image into a numeric code. Do not request<br>   manual browser takeover when approval on another device is sufficient. After<br>   the user confirms, inspect the current page and continue authentication.<br>   Repeat the choice step at each new authentication or recovery decision<br>   point. If the selected method fails, inspect the website-surfaced error<br>   before requesting credentials again. For an incorrect username, password,<br>   or verification code, report the error and, if this browser's guidance<br>   permits manual takeover, offer its documented handoff. Otherwise, let the<br>   user choose whether to retry or use a visible alternative. Never switch<br>   methods without the user's choice. If the site explicitly blocks sign-in or<br>   reports a generic failure such as "An error occurred" or "Something went<br>   wrong," stop after the first occurrence and explain that the website might<br>   be blocking sign-in. When using Cloud Browser, share the Help Center article<br>   in its guidance.<br>4. After every authentication transition, call<br>   `nodeRepl.write(await tab.dom_cua.get_visible_dom())` to inspect the rendered<br>   interactive structure across nested and cross-origin frames. Check for a<br>   CAPTCHA, error, next authentication step, or success. If the inspection<br>   appears incomplete, use a frame-aware inspection and interaction path; do not<br>   continue, assume success, or dismiss an overlay.<br>5. When authentication appears complete, verify the target site with fresh<br>   visible evidence. Treat a closed auth popup, blank page, spinner, missing tab,<br>   stale tab, or timeout as an unknown result, not a failed login and not proof<br>   of success.<br>6. If the target page fails to load after authentication, immediately create a<br>   new agent tab and navigate it to the retained target origin or canonical<br>   signed-in URL: | Cloud-environment assumption in otherwise included capability guidance. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_browser/dist/skill/references/capabilities/tab/browserAuth.md:49-112` | Drop cloud-only cross-reference/sentence, preserve the included capability and site-safety rules. |
| documentation text edit | references/capabilities/tab/browserAuth.md — Request credentials for a validated login form. Orbit's `otpHandle` can supply a short reference from a redacted email for private autofill after one-time user approval. Declining autofill offers manual credential entry. When `submit` is omitted, a `submitted` result means the credential fields were filled successfully; inspect the resulting page to confirm that the form auto-submitted and sign-in advanced.<br>}<br>```<br> | Vendor brand prose reference (not removal of included capability). | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/cua/dist/lib/js/oai_js_browser/dist/skill/references/capabilities/tab/browserAuth.md:354-357` | Rename vendor product name to cua only; preserve non-excluded prose. Orbit browserAuth remains browserAuth, not vendor account API. |

Do not infer account details that are not visible or rank or<br>   choose a method for the user.<br>3. Follow the selected method through the visible page. Use<br>   `browserAuth.request(...)` for sign-in method choices and whenever the<br>   chosen method requires user-provided credentials.<br>   If the website displays a QR code for approval on another device, call<br>   `browserAuth.request({ origin: new URL(await tab.url()).origin, fields: [], qr_code: true })`.<br>   Browser-client securely captures and decodes the visible QR code. Never<br>   inspect, print, copy, or reconstruct its destination URL yourself. The only<br>   exception is a trusted native-mobile handoff error that explicitly provides<br>   a validated HTTPS sign-in URL: show the user that exact supplied URL, ask<br>   them to open it and report back when finished, and wait for their reply.<br>   Never expose another QR payload or derive a URL the error did not supply.<br>   If two-step verification or device approval displays a number-matching<br>   challenge, tell the user the exact non-secret number or matching detail to<br>   select on their device, for example, "Tap 37 on your phone."<br>   If the matching detail is an emoji, icon, or image, describe that exact<br>   visual cue, for example, "Tap the 🥶 emoji on your phone." Never invent a<br>   number or translate an image into a numeric code. Do not request<br>   manual browser takeover when approval on another device is sufficient. After<br>   the user confirms, inspect the current page and continue authentication.<br>   Repeat the choice step at each new authentication or recovery decision<br>   point. If the selected method fails, inspect the website-surfaced error<br>   before requesting credentials again. For an incorrect username, password,<br>   or verification code, report the error and, if this browser's guidance<br>   permits manual takeover, offer its documented handoff. Otherwise, let the<br>   user choose whether to retry or use a visible alternative. Never switch<br>   methods without the user's choice. If the site explicitly blocks sign-in or<br>   reports a generic failure such as "An error occurred" or "Something went<br>   wrong," stop after the first occurrence and explain that the website might<br>   be blocking sign-in. When using Cloud Browser, share the Help Center article<br>   in its guidance.<br>4. After every authentication transition, call<br>   `nodeRepl.write(await tab.dom_cua.get_visible_dom())` to inspect the rendered<br>   interactive structure across nested and cross-origin frames. Check for a<br>   CAPTCHA, error, next authentication step, or success. If the inspection<br>   appears incomplete, use a frame-aware inspection and interaction path; do not<br>   continue, assume success, or dismiss an overlay.<br>5. When authentication appears complete, verify the target site with fresh<br>   visible evidence. Treat a closed auth popup, blank page, spinner, missing tab,<br>   stale tab, or timeout as an unknown result, not a failed login and not proof<br>   of success.<br>6. If the target page fails to load after authentication, immediately create a<br>   new agent tab and navigate it to the retained target origin or canonical<br>   signed-in URL: | Cloud-environment assumption in otherwise included capability guidance. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:1901`; `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:3735`; `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:5527`; `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:9059` | Drop cloud-only cross-reference/sentence, preserve the included capability and site-safety rules. |

| documentation text edit | browser embedded chrome-troubleshooting — ### check-native-host-manifest.js<br>Checks the shared native-host manifest in every configured Chromium browser destination and, on Windows, its shared generated `NativeMessagingHosts` registry root. It also verifies the shared native-host name and every configured extension origin. | documentation text edit | browser embedded tab-mentions-iab — # In-app Browser Tab Mentions<br>- A prompt link shaped like `plugin://browser@openai-bundled?mention=tab-v1&browserId=...&tabId=...&title=...&url=...` without `source=extension` is an explicit user mention of an open in-app browser tab. Decode its query parameters before choosing a browser or tab.<br>- Resolve each tab mention from `agent.browsers`; never assume an `iab`, `browser`, or other binding from an earlier turn still exists. If `agent.browsers` is unavailable, first run the Bootstrap block from this skill.<br>- Call `agent.browsers.list()`, select the `iab` browser whose `metadata.codexSessionId` exactly equals `browserId`, and store `await agent.browsers.get(match.id)` as a local `mentionedBrowser` handle.<br>- Call `mentionedBrowser.tabs.list()` and find the exact returned tab whose `providerTabId`, `title`, and `url` equal the decoded `tabId`, `title`, and `url`. Pass its `id` to `mentionedBrowser.tabs.get(tab.id)`.<br>- The title and URL are an accepted snapshot used to fail closed when the mentioned tab has changed. If the exact tab no longer exists or has changed, report that it is unavailable; do not silently use or open a different tab.<br>- All in-app browser tabs are available through `browser.tabs.list()` and `browser.tabs.get(id)`. Reuse an existing matching tab instead of opening a duplicate.<br> | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:1931`; `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:3765` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |

| documentation text edit | browser embedded capabilities/tab/orbitBrowserAuth — ### Authentication Lifecycle<br>1. Before the first authentication interaction, retain the target site's origin<br>   or a canonical signed-in URL for later verification, for example with<br>   `const targetOrigin = new URL(await tab.url()).origin`.<br>2. Inspect the visible page. Use only methods the page actually offers, such as<br>   phone or SMS OTP, email or Gmail OTP, Google sign-in, username and password,<br>   or device approval. Honor the user's stated identity and method<br>   preference. Reuse relevant memory and available account context for their<br>   username, email, or phone number; do not ask them to repeat known information.<br>   Autofill known identifiers through ordinary browser interactions. On an<br>   identifier-only step, ask for an unknown identifier in chat. If the current<br>   form asks for both an unknown identifier and a password, collect both in one<br>   `browserAuth.request(...)` instead of asking separately in chat.<br>   When the user supplies an identifier for the current sign-in, that is explicit<br>   authorization to fill it; do not ask for another confirmation before entering<br>   it. If the intended identity is ambiguous, ask a concise question in chat.<br>   On an account chooser, when one already signed-in account is offered, select<br>   it directly unless it conflicts with the user's stated identity or preference.<br>   Do not ask whether they want to use a different account. With multiple<br>   accounts, select a clear match to the intended identity; ask in chat if the<br>   choice remains ambiguous.<br>   Without a stated method preference, choose the offered path that needs the<br>   least user intervention: prefer Google sign-in with an already signed-in<br>   account, then email or phone OTP. Make these selections directly. When the<br>   intended method remains ambiguous, present the visible methods using the<br>   sign-in option request below.<br>   You may open Google sign-in to check for an already signed-in account; if it<br>   instead requires a fresh Google login, return to the service's other sign-in<br>   options rather than defaulting to Google.<br>3. Follow the selected method through the visible page. Handle identifiers as<br>   described above. Retrieve email OTP handles only from the connected mailbox<br>   matching the chosen identity and current sign-in attempt. For<br>   `[otp-handle:VALUE]`, pass the short reference `VALUE` as `otpHandle` to<br>   `browserAuth.request(...)`. It is scoped to this thread; never decode it or<br>   type it into the page. Each autofill asks for user approval; declining offers<br>   manual entry. Do not retry autofill after a denial. If no handle is available,<br>   ask for the code in chat. Enter user-provided codes through ordinary browser<br>   interactions. Use a code only for the current attempt; do not echo or save it.<br>   For Google verification, select an existing phone OTP method first, then<br>   device approval. Do not ask the user to choose when that selection is clear.<br>   Use `browserAuth.request(...)` for method choices, reusable secrets, email<br>   OTPs, or the QR approval flow below. Include an unknown identifier with the<br>   password when both are requested on the same form; omit identifiers already<br>   filled.<br>   Do not use `browserAuth` to create accounts. If the flow requires new account<br>   creation, offer manual handoff so the user can complete signup.<br>   If the website displays a QR code for approval on another device, call<br>   `browserAuth.request({ origin: new URL(await tab.url()).origin, fields: [], qr_code: true })`.<br>   Browser-client securely captures and decodes the visible QR code. Never<br>   inspect, print, copy, or reconstruct its destination URL yourself. The only<br>   exception is a trusted native-mobile handoff error that explicitly provides<br>   a validated HTTPS sign-in URL: show the user that exact supplied URL, ask<br>   them to open it and report back when finished, and wait for their reply.<br>   Never expose another QR payload or derive a URL the error did not supply.<br>   If two-step verification or device approval displays a number-matching<br>   challenge, tell the user the exact non-secret number or matching detail to<br>   select on their device, for example, "Tap 37 on your phone."<br>   If the matching detail is an emoji, icon, or image, describe that exact<br>   visual cue, for example, "Tap the 🥶 emoji on your phone." Never invent a<br>   number or translate an image into a numeric code. Do not request<br>   manual browser takeover when approval on another device is sufficient. After<br>   the user confirms, inspect the current page and continue authentication.<br>   Repeat the account and method selection rules at each new authentication or<br>   recovery decision point. If the selected method fails, inspect the<br>   website-surfaced error before requesting credentials again. For an incorrect<br>   username, password, or verification code, report the error and, if this browser's guidance<br>   permits manual takeover, offer its documented handoff. Otherwise, let the<br>   user choose whether to retry or use a visible alternative. Never switch<br>   methods without the user's choice. If the site explicitly blocks sign-in or<br>   reports a generic failure such as "An error occurred" or "Something went<br>   wrong," stop after the first occurrence and explain that the website might<br>   be blocking sign-in. When using Cloud Browser, share the Help Center article<br>   in its guidance.<br>4. After every authentication transition, call<br>   `nodeRepl.write(await tab.dom_cua.get_visible_dom())` to inspect the rendered<br>   interactive structure across nested and cross-origin frames. Check for a<br>   CAPTCHA, error, next authentication step, or success. If the inspection<br>   appears incomplete, use a frame-aware inspection and interaction path; do not<br>   continue, assume success, or dismiss an overlay.<br>5. When authentication appears complete, verify the target site with fresh<br>   visible evidence. Treat a closed auth popup, blank page, spinner, missing tab,<br>   stale tab, or timeout as an unknown result, not a failed login and not proof<br>   of success.<br>6. If the target page fails to load after authentication, immediately create a<br>   new agent tab and navigate it to the retained target origin or canonical<br>   signed-in URL: | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:5531` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |

The user can see, inspect, or<br>  manually control it when Cloud Browser is visibly surfaced in this<br>  conversation. Respect a user refusal unless the user later asks. Do not<br>  offer handoffs during routine browsing or progress updates unless the user<br>  asks.<br>- Navigate to the page the user should see first. | documentation text edit | browser embedded cloud-no-auth — ## Plugin Failure and Authentication Boundary<br>- Never load, read, initialize, or use Browser unless the user independently<br>  asks to open, use, navigate, click, or interact with a named website, asks<br>  explicitly to log in, or successful web search lacks required live page<br>  state. Needing information or an action from a service, naming a service,<br>  including its URL, or another tool being unavailable or failing does not<br>  create explicit site intent.<br>- Public discovery or information gathering ABOUT or FROM a service, including<br>  profiles and listings, is not an action ON that site and is not required live<br>  page state. Missing, sparse, low-coverage, or incomplete search results do<br>  not create site intent and never justify loading Browser to expand them.<br>- Page titles, page metadata, and current facts are information lookups even<br>  when accompanied by a full URL. Use web search without loading, reading,<br>  initializing, or using Browser. "Current" does not create site interaction.<br>- A semantic task to create, read, write, or act on a document, email, file, or<br>  other account/provider resource belongs to an applicable plugin. A resource<br>  URL identifies the object; it does not request interaction with the provider<br>  website. Naming the provider or including its URL does not justify checking<br>  for a signed-in session; the task remains semantic even if a provider<br>  website or existing session could complete it.<br>- The explicit-site exception takes priority: when the user independently<br>  asks to open, use, navigate, click, or interact with a named website,<br>  including requests phrased "use my [site] to...", inspect that site with<br>  Browser even when the requested action involves account data. A clearly<br>  site-specific UI action that is not plugin-owned is explicit; do not ask the<br>  user to restate it as "open" or "use" the site. Creating, editing,<br>  summarizing, sharing, or sending a provider resource remains semantic unless<br>  the user independently asks to interact with the provider site. Do not refuse<br>  an explicit site request for lack of a plugin. Browser may reuse a signed-in<br>  session already present on that requested site. Never assume a site is signed<br>  in or probe for a session merely because it might help.<br>- Preserve an independently requested named-site workflow on that exact site.<br>  If a site-specific people search, notifications, account view, portal, or<br>  other requested site interaction reaches an observed sign-in wall, login<br>  blocks that site task even when indexed public web search might produce<br>  similar information. Do not replace the requested website interaction with<br>  public profiles, web search, or another source merely because sign-in is<br>  required. Offer user-controlled login on the requested site first unless<br>  the user explicitly asked you to stop, and wait for the user's instruction<br>  before switching to a different source. Generic public discovery that did<br>  not independently request website interaction remains search-first.<br>- Browser is never a recovery path for an unavailable or failed plugin. Surface<br>  the limitation and use another non-browser path. Do not load, read,<br>  initialize, or use Browser, open the provider's website, or probe for a<br>  session solely to work around the failure. Do not suggest asking for Browser,<br>  opening the provider website, or logging in as a workaround for an implicit<br>  task or a failed plugin.<br>- Continue with Browser only for that independently requested website<br>  interaction or when the task already required live public site-specific<br>  state and Browser was not selected because another plugin failed.<br>- For an explicit login or sign-in request, initialize Browser and navigate<br>  only to the requested site's verified sign-in page. This runtime cannot<br>  perform a credential handoff. Stop before entering or<br>  submitting any credentials. If the user explicitly asked you to stop at<br>  sign-in, stop without offering manual takeover. Otherwise, say: "You can<br>  manually log in using Cloud Browser within this conversation on chatgpt.com.<br>  Do not paste or type your credentials into chat." Never advertise that path<br>  for an implicit account task or an unavailable or failed plugin.<br>- If an independently requested website interaction reaches an observed<br>  sign-in wall that blocks the requested task, stop without offering manual<br>  takeover if the user explicitly asked you to stop at sign-in. Otherwise,<br>  tell them they can sign in themselves using Cloud Browser within this<br>  conversation on chatgpt.com, then tell you when to continue. Do not paste or<br>  type credentials into chat. Stop automation before any credential entry and<br>  do not inspect or interact with the page during manual sign-in. Once the user<br>  asks you to continue, inspect only the final page state and resume the<br>  original task. An implicit account task, an unavailable or failed plugin, or<br>  unsuccessful web search never justifies opening a site or offering manual<br>  takeover.<br>- When reporting the signed-out state itself completes the requested task, do<br>  not offer sign-in or manual takeover. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:5539`; `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:7332` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |
| documentation text edit | browser embedded cloud-shared-files — If the user uploads a file, or if Codex generates a file that the browser must use, put it under `/workspace/scratch` in the main container. The matching relative path is synchronized under `/home/oai/share` in the browser container. For example, `/workspace/scratch/report.pdf` corresponds to `/home/oai/share/report.pdf`. | Inside excluded file/document; remove with that unit. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:5541` | Drop this exact paragraph with the excluded document; do not transplant cloud/desktop instructions. |

| documentation text edit | browser embedded capabilities/tab/browserAuth — ### Authentication Lifecycle<br>1. Before the first authentication interaction, retain the target site's origin<br>   or a canonical signed-in URL for later verification, for example with<br>   `const targetOrigin = new URL(await tab.url()).origin`.<br>2. Inspect the visible page. Use only methods the page actually offers, such as<br>   phone or SMS OTP, email or Gmail OTP, Google sign-in, username and password,<br>   or device approval. Honor the user's stated identity and method<br>   preference. Reuse relevant memory and available account context for their<br>   username, email, or phone number; do not ask them to repeat known information.<br>   Autofill known identifiers through ordinary browser interactions. On an<br>   identifier-only step, ask for an unknown identifier in chat. If the current<br>   form asks for both an unknown identifier and a password, collect both in one<br>   `browserAuth.request(...)` instead of asking separately in chat.<br>   When the user supplies an identifier for the current sign-in, that is explicit<br>   authorization to fill it; do not ask for another confirmation before entering<br>   it. If the intended identity is ambiguous, ask a concise question in chat.<br>   On an account chooser, when one already signed-in account is offered, select<br>   it directly unless it conflicts with the user's stated identity or preference.<br>   Do not ask whether they want to use a different account. With multiple<br>   accounts, select a clear match to the intended identity; ask in chat if the<br>   choice remains ambiguous.<br>   Without a stated method preference, choose the offered path that needs the<br>   least user intervention. You may explore the offered sign-in methods to check<br>   for an already signed-in account with an identity provider, such as Google,<br>   even when the target site is signed out. When you find one, use it directly<br>   under the account-selection rules above, without another confirmation.<br>   Prefer an already signed-in account, then email or phone OTP. If a provider<br>   instead requires a fresh login, return to the service's other sign-in options.<br>   Make these selections directly. When the intended method remains ambiguous,<br>   present the visible methods using the sign-in option request below.<br>3. Follow the selected method through the visible page. Handle identifiers as<br>   described above. Retrieve email OTP handles only from the connected mailbox<br>   matching the chosen identity and current sign-in attempt. For<br>   `[otp-handle:VALUE]`, pass the short reference `VALUE` as `otpHandle` to<br>   `browserAuth.request(...)`. It is scoped to this thread; never decode it or<br>   type it into the page. Each autofill asks for user approval; declining offers<br>   manual entry. Do not retry autofill after a denial. If no handle is available,<br>   ask for the code in chat. Enter user-provided codes through ordinary browser<br>   interactions. Use a code only for the current attempt; do not echo or save it.<br>   For Google verification, select an existing phone OTP method first, then<br>   device approval. Do not ask the user to choose when that selection is clear.<br>   Use `browserAuth.request(...)` for method choices, reusable secrets, email<br>   OTPs, or the QR approval flow below. Include an unknown identifier with the<br>   password when both are requested on the same form; omit identifiers already<br>   filled.<br>   Do not use `browserAuth` to create accounts. If the flow requires new account<br>   creation, offer manual handoff so the user can complete signup.<br>   If the website displays a QR code for approval on another device, call<br>   `browserAuth.request({ origin: new URL(await tab.url()).origin, fields: [], qr_code: true })`.<br>   Browser-client securely captures and decodes the visible QR code. Never<br>   inspect, print, copy, or reconstruct its destination URL yourself. The only<br>   exception is a trusted native-mobile handoff error that explicitly provides<br>   a validated HTTPS sign-in URL: show the user that exact supplied URL, ask<br>   them to open it and report back when finished, and wait for their reply.<br>   Never expose another QR payload or derive a URL the error did not supply.<br>   If two-step verification or device approval displays a number-matching<br>   challenge, tell the user the exact non-secret number or matching detail to<br>   select on their device, for example, "Tap 37 on your phone."<br>   If the matching detail is an emoji, icon, or image, describe that exact<br>   visual cue, for example, "Tap the 🥶 emoji on your phone." Never invent a<br>   number or translate an image into a numeric code. Do not request<br>   manual browser takeover when approval on another device is sufficient. After<br>   the user confirms, call `await tab.getAXState()` and continue authentication.<br>   Repeat the account and method selection rules at each new authentication or<br>   recovery decision point. If the selected method fails, inspect the<br>   website-surfaced error before requesting credentials again. For an incorrect<br>   username, password, or verification code, report the error and, if this browser's guidance<br>   permits manual takeover, offer its documented handoff. Otherwise, let the<br>   user choose whether to retry or use a visible alternative. Never switch<br>   methods without the user's choice. If the site explicitly blocks sign-in or<br>   reports a generic failure such as "An error occurred" or "Something went<br>   wrong," stop after the first occurrence and explain that the website might<br>   be blocking sign-in. When using Cloud Browser, share the Help Center article<br>   in its guidance.<br>4. After every authentication transition, call<br>   `await tab.getAXState()`. Check for a CAPTCHA, error, next authentication<br>   step, or success. If AX state is incomplete, call `await tab.getScreenshot()`<br>   and use frame-aware Playwright inspection where needed. Do not assume success<br>   or dismiss an overlay without inspecting it.<br>5. When authentication appears complete, verify the target site with fresh<br>   `await tab.getAXState()` evidence. Treat a closed auth popup, blank page,<br>   spinner, missing tab, stale tab, or timeout as an unknown result, not a failed<br>   login and not proof of success.<br>6. If the target page fails to load after authentication, immediately create a<br>   new agent tab and navigate it to the retained target origin or canonical<br>   signed-in URL: | Cloud-environment assumption in otherwise included capability guidance. | `chatgpt-26.928.40906/cua_node/@oai/browser-desktop/scripts/browser-service.mjs:7322` | Drop cloud-only cross-reference/sentence, preserve the included capability and site-safety rules. |

## Table 3 — DOCUMENT TEXT

Actions apply to the cited text unit, not a wholesale rewrite of its parent section. `keep-verbatim` means unchanged surviving prose; `rename-only` means vendor product names → cua, with independently enumerated excluded clauses removed; `drop` can target a named clause within an otherwise retained section. Embedded duplicate catalogs share a row when their bytes match. Keep the confirmations policy even when it mentions cloud data, accounts or telemetry as user-data categories. Keep additional capability documentation discoverable; do not emit all capability documents at first call.
